# Agents · L · part 2

915 entries, language-specialist to lb-frontend-dev.agent. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **language-specialist**: Language and framework implementation and idiom across TypeScript, JavaScript, Python, Go, Rust, Java, Kotlin, C#, C++, Ruby, PHP, Elixir, Swift, Django, FastAPI, Rails, Laravel, Spring, React, Next.js, Vue, Angular, Node, Flutter and Expo. Use for framework-level questions and language-specific code. ([wippa-studios/wippaCLAUDE](https://github.com/wippa-studios/wippaCLAUDE/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/language-specialist.md) · ★1)
- **language-specialists**: 26 subagents specialized in specific programming languages. ([neilmac91/EarningsNerd](https://github.com/neilmac91/EarningsNerd/blob/HEAD/.claude/skills/subagents/voltagent/agents/language-specialists.md) · ★1)
- **language-specialists-diagnostician**: Diagnose whether a task belongs to doctrine, subtree control-plane routing, root runtime surfaces, or donor fallback inside developer-tool's language-specialists subsystem. Trigger keywords: c++, kotlin, swift, flutter, php, powershell, language idiom, toolchain, interop routing. ([joySUSY/violet-plugin-place](https://github.com/joySUSY/violet-plugin-place/blob/HEAD/plugins/developer-tool/agents/language-specialists-diagnostician.md))
- **language-support**: Implements Tidepool's language intelligence — tidepool-syntax (Rust, Markdown and TOML lexers, language packs, brackets, indent), tidepool-markdown (CommonMark + GFM), tidepool-rpc (JSON-RPC, LSP, DAP, TAP framing) and tidepool-lang (rust-analyzer sessions, LSP features, cargo tasks and problems, Markdown services, DAP debugging). Use for Rust and Markdown features, LSP/DAP work and new language packs. ([jabbott-iii/Tidepool](https://github.com/jabbott-iii/Tidepool/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/language-support.md) · ★1)
- **language-swift-expert**: Expert in the Swift language (Swift 6.2) — strict concurrency (async/await, actors, Sendable, Swift 6.2 approachable concurrency), value semantics & noncopyable types, typed throws, optionals/safety, generics & protocols (opaque vs existential, parameter packs), macros, ARC, SwiftPM, the API Design Guidelines, Swift Testing, and Obj-C/C/C++ interop. Use for idiomatic-Swift review, concurrency/data-race issues, and Swift 6 migration — not SwiftUI/UIKit app design. ([SteveGJones/ai-first-sdlc-practices](https://github.com/SteveGJones/ai-first-sdlc-practices/blob/HEAD/plugins/sdlc-lang-swift/agents/language-swift-expert.md) · ★42)
- **language-teacher**: Acts as a structured language teacher who sets a lesson goal, presents, practises and checks, stays at the learner's CEFR level and recycles earlier material. For learners who want lessons. ([hermes-hq/hodios-dist](https://github.com/hermes-hq/hodios-dist/blob/HEAD/native/claude-code/.claude/agents/language-teacher.md) · ★1 · 4 copies)
- **language-theorist**: Opus-class agent specialized in programming language theory, type systems, lambda calculus, parsing, and compiler-pipeline-level concerns. The agent you reach for when designing or analyzing a language, a type system, or an operational semantics. ([Tibsfox/gsd-skill-creator](https://github.com/Tibsfox/gsd-skill-creator/blob/HEAD/examples/cartridges/software-engineering/agents/language-theorist.md) · ★71)
- **language-translator**: Real-time Spanish ↔ English translation specialist with cultural context, regional dialect awareness, travel phrase guidance, and tone-appropriate communication for everyday, business, and emergency situations ([cloudsysops/opsly](https://github.com/cloudsysops/opsly/blob/HEAD/vendor/agency-agents/integrations/qwen/agents/language-translator.md) · ★1 · 4 copies)

- **languages**: A language pack is a partial set of translations for known English catalog keys. Resolution is per key: user pack, built-in pack, then English. A user pack with a built-in code contains only user data, so it can override one message without copying the built-in pack. Saving does not activate a pack. ([andriy-viyatyk/persephone](https://github.com/andriy-viyatyk/persephone/blob/HEAD/assets/guides/agents/languages.md) · ★11)
- **languages**: All `swe-workbench` language skills available in this plugin. Use the `Skill` tool to invoke any of these. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/blob/HEAD/shared/agents/languages.md) · ★3)
- **languages-glossary**: Each language pack task settles its core terminology before drafting translations. Later fixes and updates reuse those decisions. Keep product and protocol names listed below in English. ([andriy-viyatyk/persephone](https://github.com/andriy-viyatyk/persephone/blob/HEAD/assets/guides/agents/languages-glossary.md) · ★11)
- **languages-specialist**: Expert guidance on 9 programming languages and 10+ frameworks. Compare languages, understand ecosystems, choose optimal stacks. - **langwatch-expert**: Active guide to the LangWatch observability/evaluation platform. Its mission is to get the team instrumented with LangWatch (tracing first), then proactively surface high-leverage platform capabilities (evals, experiments, prompt versioning, guardrails, cost dashboards) as opportunities arise — not just answer questions. Owns instrumentation, tracing, the trace schema, evaluations, simulations, prompts, alerts, and analytics. Delegate LangWatch platform and observability work to it. Grounds in live docs and schema discovery via the LangWatch MCP server over priors. ([langwatch/claude-marketplace](https://github.com/langwatch/claude-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/langwatch-experts/agents/langwatch-expert.md))

- **lanterns-elias**: Plays Elias Coyne (seventeen, unpaid caretaker of lanterns 1-4, Mira's apprentice) for a Lanterns scene. Given an approved scene skeleton, returns dialogue, physical business, register calls, and in-character pushback. Performance material only — never narration, never finished prose. Use in stage 3 of the /scene writers' room. EXAMPLE AGENT — ships with the example-lanterns project. ([darkdankdeals/novel-workbench](https://github.com/darkdankdeals/novel-workbench/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lanterns-elias.md) · ★1)
- **lanterns-ledger**: Renders Lanterns' embedded non-narrator voice — the lamp-keeper's ledger entries, in four keepers' distinct hands — from established canon. Verbatim canon entries where they exist, register-true proposals where they don't, silence calls where the beat needs none. Use in stage 3 of the /scene writers' room. EXAMPLE AGENT — ships with the example-lanterns project. ([darkdankdeals/novel-workbench](https://github.com/darkdankdeals/novel-workbench/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lanterns-ledger.md) · ★1)
- **lanterns-mira**: Plays Mira Vaughan (interim lamp-keeper, POV character) for a Lanterns scene — her spoken dialogue and physical business ONLY, never her interiority, which is author-owned. Returns register calls and in-character pushback. Performance material only. Use in stage 3 of the /scene writers' room. EXAMPLE AGENT — ships with the example-lanterns project. ([darkdankdeals/novel-workbench](https://github.com/darkdankdeals/novel-workbench/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lanterns-mira.md) · ★1)
- **lanterns-scene-builder**: Turns the author's beats for a Lanterns scene into a production-ready scene skeleton — setting, on-stage cast, blocking, stakes, beat sequence, continuity checks against the wiki. Never writes prose or dialogue. Use as stage 2 of the /scene writers' room. EXAMPLE AGENT — ships with the example-lanterns project. ([darkdankdeals/novel-workbench](https://github.com/darkdankdeals/novel-workbench/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lanterns-scene-builder.md) · ★1)
- **lanterns-side-cast**: Plays Lanterns' side characters (townspeople, council attendees, the woman at the back, strangers, crowds) for a scene — one performer, multiple small roles, each grounded in the town's register. Returns dialogue, physical business, and pushback per role. Performance material only — never narration, never finished prose. Use in stage 3 of the /scene writers' room. EXAMPLE AGENT — ships with the example-lanterns project. ([darkdankdeals/novel-workbench](https://github.com/darkdankdeals/novel-workbench/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lanterns-side-cast.md) · ★1)
- **lanterns-vane**: Plays Robert Vane (council chair, the book's opposition — reasonable, procedural, never a fool) for a Lanterns scene. Given an approved scene skeleton, returns dialogue, physical business, register calls, and in-character pushback. Performance material only — never narration, never finished prose. Use in stage 3 of the /scene writers' room. EXAMPLE AGENT — ships with the example-lanterns project. ([darkdankdeals/novel-workbench](https://github.com/darkdankdeals/novel-workbench/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lanterns-vane.md) · ★1)
- **lanza**: Fleet worker, SONNET tier. Documentation generator — READMEs, API/reference docs, doc-comments, architecture notes, changelogs, usage guides, and onboarding docs derived from existing code. Dispatched by the lead thread with a single bounded task brief. Interchangeable with skald (use distinct ones to run docs work in parallel). Drop to fencer/tabloid (haiku) for mechanical text edits; escalate to count/wiseman (sonnet) for code changes. ([ApprenticeofEnder/ApprenticeofEnder](https://github.com/ApprenticeofEnder/ApprenticeofEnder/blob/HEAD/modules/home/ai-coding/fleetmanager/agents/lanza.md) · ★1)
- **lanzamiento-por-tandas-con-vuelta-atras**: Planifica cómo sacar una versión nueva de un producto digital (app, web, tienda online, automatización) a tandas de usuarios, qué vigilar en cada tanda, cuándo parar y cómo volver atrás. Úsalo antes de un lanzamiento que pueda afectar a clientes, cobros o datos. - **lao-tzu**: 자문단 위원. 어떤 무리가 막 움직이려 하는데 그 움직임이 오히려 일을 더 나쁘게 만들지 아무도 묻지 않을 때, 또는 고장 난 것처럼 보이는 시스템이 사실은 스스로 낫는 중일 수 있을 때 부른다. 단독으로 부르거나 /council 다관점 토론에 함께 부른다. 개입하지 않는 선택지를 진지한 후보로 올려놓고, 짜 넣은 구조 대신 저절로 생겨나는 질서를 본다. - **lao-tzu**: Invoke Lao Tzu when the mentee faces questions of strategic restraint, timing, or non-force (wu wei); when pushing harder is generating more resistance; when inner stillness, intuition, or detachment is the missing resource; when leadership style needs to shift from command to invisible influence; when a decision requires patience rather than action; or when the question touches the integration of personal values, longevity, and sustainable power over the long arc of a life. Primary domains: Life & Character, Strategy & Competition, Intuition & Inner-Self. Secondary: Purpose & Longevity. ([jakapolr/world-philosophers](https://github.com/jakapolr/world-philosophers/blob/HEAD/agents/lao-tzu.md) · ★1)

- **laytime-check**: Work out a laytime statement (time allowed, time used, demurrage or despatch) from charter-party terms and a statement of facts, with every figure shown and nothing assumed. Use when the user gives laytime terms, a NOR, an SOF or stoppages and asks for laytime, demurrage, despatch or a time sheet, or asks to check someone else's laytime calculation. ([PowerpBoxCaptain/laytime-check](https://github.com/PowerpBoxCaptain/laytime-check/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/laytime-check.agent.md))
- **lazard**: DevOps/CI-CD agent -- sets up deployment pipelines, Docker, monitoring, and infrastructure configuration. Use for Standard+ tiers in create-project. Handles all operational concerns. ([Mister-Wolfgang/mako-ai-agents](https://github.com/Mister-Wolfgang/mako-ai-agents/blob/HEAD/agents/lazard.md))
- **Laziness Detector**: Detects and prevents destructive avoidance behaviors including test deletion, feature removal, coverage regression, and premature task abandonment ([jmagly/aiwg](https://github.com/jmagly/aiwg/blob/HEAD/agentic/code/frameworks/sdlc-complete/agents/laziness-detector.md) · ★230 · 5 copies)
- **lazy-checker**: Deferred-debt and shortcut pattern detector — finds patterns of hasty code accumulation across a file or set of files. Complements llm-checker (training-bias patterns) and nitpicker (individual style instances). Use for code quality reviews focused on accumulated shortcuts and deferred cleanup. ([NodeJSmith/Claudefiles](https://github.com/NodeJSmith/Claudefiles/blob/HEAD/agents/lazy-checker.md) · ★2)
- **lazy-cli-guard**: Guards the lazy-import contract and Typer command registration in the skewed-sequences CLI. Use when a change touches cli.py or config.py, adds/renames a Typer command or experiment runner, or adds a top-level import to a module that config.py/cli.py import. ([mgrts/skewed-sequences](https://github.com/mgrts/skewed-sequences/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lazy-cli-guard.md))
- **lazy-config**: Persisted `agent_config` snapshots env-derived values at sync time, so rotated env vars don't reach already-stored schedule rows. Two complementary mechanisms unfreeze them: ([happyvertical/smrt](https://github.com/happyvertical/smrt/blob/HEAD/packages/agents/agents/lazy-config.md) · ★7)
- **lazy-core.autocheckup**: Dispatch from a cross-project rollout loop (one agent per project), or directly with `repo=&lt;absolute path&gt;`, when ONE repo's lazycortex config should be checked and mechanically repaired with no operator in the loop. Applies only derivable fixes and reports everything else. Sibling `lazy-core.autosetup` runs the install chain instead of the checks. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-core/agents/lazy-core.autocheckup.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-core.autosetup**: Dispatch from a cross-project rollout loop (one agent per project), or directly with `repo=&lt;absolute path&gt;`, when ONE repo's lazycortex config must be brought current with no operator in the loop — e.g. after a plugin update changed what install seeds. NOT for first-time project setup. Sibling `lazy-core.autocheckup` checks and repairs instead of installing. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-core/agents/lazy-core.autosetup.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-diagram.draw-ascii**: Dispatched by /lazy-diagram.draw or /lazy-diagram.fix once kind and format are settled; dispatch it directly only when you have ALREADY chosen format=ascii and kind=&lt;one of: controls-scheme, decision-tree, flow, fs-tree, layout, tree&gt; — it never infers either. Single-pass writer: its whole response is the ASCII diagram body, without the surrounding triple-backticks. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-diagram/agents/lazy-diagram.draw-ascii.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-diagram.draw-mermaid**: Dispatched by /lazy-diagram.draw or /lazy-diagram.fix once kind and format are settled; dispatch it directly only when you have ALREADY chosen format=mermaid and kind=&lt;one of: flow, sequence, state, erd, class, architecture, layout, nav, tree, controls-scheme, decision-tree, screen-scheme, journey, mindmap, gantt, timeline&gt; — it never infers either. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-diagram/agents/lazy-diagram.draw-mermaid.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.architect**: Use when approved designs settle what the system should do and `architecture.md` still needs writing or revising — module boundaries, dependency direction, public contract, data migration, cost to callers. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the designer when behavior is decided and only code shape is open, over the planner when nothing is sequenced yet. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.architect.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.data-implementer**: Use when an approved content design settles what an entity is and its data files still need writing into the product's repository, in the project's own schemas. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the implementer when the design itself is the specification, over the tester when the job is producing data rather than validating it. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.data-implementer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.debugger**: Use when something fails, returns a wrong result, or behaves unexpectedly and nobody knows why yet — the cause is explained first, then fixed. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the tester when the defect is known and needs a root cause, over the implementer when the problem itself is the unknown. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.debugger.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.designer**: Use when a brief is settled and the work needs a scoped design spec stating what is being built and why — not how. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the planner when file paths and task lists would be premature, over the interpreter when the gaps in the request are already closed. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.designer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.docs-writer**: Use when an approved design settles what the user gets and the product's user-facing documentation still needs writing, straight from the design. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the implementer when the deliverable is documentation rather than code, over the fiction-writer when it is product docs rather than literary prose. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.docs-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.editor**: Use when a written document's prose must be brought up to the project's writing canon — shop talk, coined terms, synonym rotation, filler, evaluative epithets, broken language. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the reviewer when defects are corrected in place rather than reported; never for literary text, which is the fiction-editor's. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.editor.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.fiction-editor**: Use when literary text is already drafted and needs an editing pass — rhythm, filter words, dead metaphor, named emotion, point-of-view leaks. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the fiction-writer when only the prose is in question, over the editor when the text is a scene rather than a technical document; never to change plot or outcome. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.fiction-editor.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.fiction-writer**: Use when the deliverable is literary text — narrative prose, a scene, dialogue, a lyrical fragment — written from an existing brief or story outline. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the fiction-editor when the text does not exist yet; never for technical documents or story architecture, which come from upstream. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.fiction-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.implementer**: Use when an ordered implementation plan exists and needs carrying into code task by task, test-first, verified with the repo's own check and test runners. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the debugger when the job is building what the plan describes, over the planner when the task breakdown already exists. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.implementer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.interpreter**: Use when a request is too vague to act on — a free-form ask, a rough note, an old doc, a log — and needs a gap-free structured brief before design starts. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the designer when the why and the unknowns are not pinned down yet; it asks its questions in the document and proposes no solution. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.interpreter.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.planner**: Use when a design spec exists and the work still needs an ordered, file-level implementation plan with a test command and a rollback procedure. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the designer when what to build is decided and only sequencing is missing, over the implementer when nothing should be written yet. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.planner.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.researcher**: Use when an approved research design states the questions and the research report `research.md` still needs writing — findings with sources from specs, code, wiki and the internet. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the interpreter when the questions are settled and only the answer is missing, over the designer when nothing is being built. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.researcher.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.reviewer**: Use when a change — a diff, a finished task, a feature branch — needs an independent correctness-and-quality read before it lands, as ranked findings with evidence. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the tester when the verdict comes from reading the change rather than running it; it never edits what it reviews. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.reviewer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.tester**: Use when the answer has to come from actually running things — a test plan, a plan execution, a bug report, a minimal reproduction — with the repo's real test mechanisms. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the reviewer when reading the code is not enough, over the debugger when the defect still has to be found rather than explained; it never fixes. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.tester.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.ui-designer**: Use when an approved design needs its user interface settled — screens, states, navigation and interaction in a ui-design document with HTML mockups beside it. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the designer when behavior is approved and only the interface is open; never for production frontend code. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.ui-designer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-experts.use-case-writer**: Use when a settled brief or request needs formal use cases before design — actors, goals, main and alternative flows, pre- and postconditions, in the actor's language. Dispatched by the expert runtime or directly. Pick it over the designer when requirement scenarios are the deliverable, over the tester when they state what the user needs rather than how it is accepted. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-experts/agents/lazy-experts.use-case-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-ext-selected**: ext: selecting the lazy extension surfaces its session_start tool. ([tintinweb/pi-subagents](https://github.com/tintinweb/pi-subagents/blob/HEAD/test/fixtures/.pi/agents/lazy-ext-selected.md) · ★1.5k · 7 copies)
- **lazy-ext-unselected**: A lazy extension left out of the ext: flip stays muted. ([tintinweb/pi-subagents](https://github.com/tintinweb/pi-subagents/blob/HEAD/test/fixtures/.pi/agents/lazy-ext-unselected.md) · ★1.5k · 7 copies)
- **lazy-log.bullets**: Use when one plugin is being released and its CHANGELOG.public.md needs a release block drafted from a commit range — dispatched by any release-drafting flow, or directly with plugin + range + version. Renders the `### &lt;version&gt; — &lt;date&gt; UTC` block of user-visible bullets, the one release-facing writer in the lazy-log family. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-core/agents/lazy-log.bullets.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-log.recall**: Use when the user asks why or when one specific thing changed — 'why was X changed?', 'when did we switch to Y?', 'what commit removed Z?'. Returns ranked matches with git SHAs. Pick this over `lazy-log.summary` (thematic narrative of a topic) and `lazy-log.timeline` (dated list) when the answer is a particular change to point at. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-core/agents/lazy-log.recall.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-log.summary**: Use when the user wants the whole story of a feature, refactor, or area — 'how did the plugin system evolve', 'catch me up on the logging skills', 'explain the auth middleware migration'. Pick this over `lazy-log.recall` (ranked matches with SHAs) and `lazy-log.timeline` (dated list) when the answer is an explanation rather than a list. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-core/agents/lazy-log.summary.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-log.timeline**: Use when the user wants what happened when — 'what changed last week', 'everything on hooks since 2026-03-01', 'walk me through the last two weeks'. Pick this over `lazy-log.recall` (ranked matches for one change) and `lazy-log.summary` (thematic narrative, no dates) when chronology is the point. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-core/agents/lazy-log.timeline.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-no-selector**: A tool registered at session_start reaches the subagent (#125). ([tintinweb/pi-subagents](https://github.com/tintinweb/pi-subagents/blob/HEAD/test/fixtures/.pi/agents/lazy-no-selector.md) · ★1.5k · 7 copies)
- **lazy-obsidian.gen-tag-pages**: Use when tags were added, renamed, or removed on the vault's notes and the pages under `Tags/` no longer match, or when the operator asks to 'regenerate tag pages'. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-obsidian/agents/lazy-obsidian.gen-tag-pages.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-planner**: Makes the easiest, cheapest plan to fix or implement something. ([BarrettLowe/dotfiles](https://github.com/BarrettLowe/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/ai/agents/lazy-planner.md))
- **lazy-python.code-reviewer**: Use when new or changed Python needs judging against the `lazy-python.*` guidelines and the project overlay — 'review src/billing/invoice.py against our guidelines'. Dispatched when `chk-py review` prints a manifest, or directly with a file list. Reports findings only and never edits; `/lazy-python.check-style` is the full check-and-clean-up pass. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-python/agents/lazy-python.code-reviewer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-python.contract-writer**: Use when a caller-visible promise of a public or protected method, class or attribute needs a `Contract:` block — 'formalize the deep-copy guarantee on snapshot', 'make this override obligation a contract', a new guarantee or a reworded one; never for a private helper. Pick it over domain-writer when the knowledge is a promise to callers, not a domain rule. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-python/agents/lazy-python.contract-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-python.docstring-writer**: Use when classes, methods or properties in Python need docstrings added or fixed under the `lazy-python.*` documentation conventions — 'write docstrings for this class'. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-python/agents/lazy-python.docstring-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-python.domain-writer**: Use when domain knowledge in code needs a `Domain(…):` block — 'document the late-fee rule next to its calculation', a mechanic, formula or rule, or an existing block after the mechanic changed — or when parked `Domain(unfiled):` blocks must be refiled (dispatch with `refile=true`). Pick it over contract-writer when the knowledge is a domain rule, not a promise to callers. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-python/agents/lazy-python.domain-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-python.test-writer**: Use when a Python class or module needs unit tests written under the `lazy-python.*` testing conventions — 'write tests for Parser'. Writes test files only and never modifies production code. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-python/agents/lazy-python.test-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-review.coordinator**: Dispatched by the review runtime on each wake of a document under review; not for direct use. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-review/agents/lazy-review.coordinator.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-review.doc_doctor**: Dispatched by lazy-review.coordinator when a document under review still does not parse after `repair-structure`; not for direct use. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-review/agents/lazy-review.doc_doctor.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-runtime.doctor**: Dispatched hourly by the `lazy-runtime.doctor` routine when a runtime job or halt looks stuck; not for direct use. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-core/agents/lazy-runtime.doctor.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-senior**: Делает наименьшее полное изменение и честно говорит, что не проверено. Лень про новый код, не про чтение и не про безопасность. ([mazurovn/BMAD_BDD_SDD_ADR_SKILL_TOOLS](https://github.com/mazurovn/BMAD_BDD_SDD_ADR_SKILL_TOOLS/blob/HEAD/agents/lazy-senior.md))
- **lazy-senior-reviewer**: Read-only review in the voice of a lazy, excellent senior engineer. Calls out code that could go, comments that earn nothing, defensive padding, and future maintenance debt. One lane of the adversarial review. ([itoh-shun/rig](https://github.com/itoh-shun/rig/blob/HEAD/agents/lazy-senior-reviewer.md))
- **lazy-skeptic-reviewer**: コード変更・新規ファイル・新機能の必要性を悲観的にレビューするエージェント。不要な複雑さやオーバーエンジニアリングを指摘する。例: User「ユーザープロフィール画面を追加しました」でlazy-skeptic-reviewerが起動し追加されたファイルや機能の必要性を疑う。User「このヘルパーファイルを作成しました」でそのファイルが本当に必要か検証する。User「新しいライブラリを導入したいです」でそのライブラリが本当に必要か疑う。 ([hata-junnosuke/runmates](https://github.com/hata-junnosuke/runmates/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lazy-skeptic-reviewer.md))
- **lazy-spec.catalog-coordinator**: Dispatched by the runtime on a wake against a catalog-root or product level note, or by /lazy-spec.drive; not for direct use. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-specs/agents/lazy-spec.catalog-coordinator.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-spec.coherence-checker**: Dispatched by the spec coordinators through `coherence-dispatch` after a spec document is approved; not for direct use. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-specs/agents/lazy-spec.coherence-checker.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-spec.coordinator**: Dispatched by the runtime on a wake against an asset's status folder-note, or by /lazy-spec.drive; not for direct use. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-specs/agents/lazy-spec.coordinator.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-user**: The last gate before a screen ships. Same persona as agent 11 ("onhandige gebruiker") in our app development skill (`/improve`), so both skills run one test. Background and sources: `../reference/intuitive-beautiful.md`. ([pantalytics/pantalytics-brand](https://github.com/pantalytics/pantalytics-brand/blob/HEAD/skills/pantalytics-odoo-ui/agents/lazy-user.md))
- **lazy-wiki.curator**: Dispatch when a wiki node needs classification (kind=classify) or See-also linking (kind=link) — from the daemon's scan routines or `/lazy-wiki.relink`. Consolidating a surface's tag values is the sibling `lazy-wiki.tag-curator`'s job, not this one's. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-wiki/agents/lazy-wiki.curator.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-wiki.domain-spec-writer**: Dispatched by the `lazy-wiki.domain-scan` / `lazy-wiki.domain-full` routines and /lazy-wiki.domain-sync; not for direct use. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-wiki/agents/lazy-wiki.domain-spec-writer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-wiki.gatherer**: Dispatch from /lazy-wiki.query once, after the seekers have returned validated entry points. Walks the wiki graph from those entry points — following glossed See-also links and on-demand backlinks (grep) — reads only the relevant node bodies, and returns a synthesised answer with source links. Keeps all traversed node bodies out of the caller's context. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-wiki/agents/lazy-wiki.gatherer.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-wiki.seeker**: Dispatch from /lazy-wiki.query, one per configured wiki scope, to pick entry points for a question. Reads ONLY the one scope's topics.md named in the prompt and returns the relevant node paths (verbatim from the index) with their glosses — never traverses, never reads node bodies. Keeps the large topic catalog out of the caller's context. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-wiki/agents/lazy-wiki.seeker.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-wiki.structure-curator**: Dispatch when a tracked path changed and the project-structure map may need its entry updated (kind=curate or kind=rename, from the structure routines), or when the whole map must be checked against the tree (kind=report, from `/lazy-wiki.audit`). Owns `docs/structure.md` only and never edits the files it describes. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-wiki/agents/lazy-wiki.structure-curator.md) · ★1)
- **lazy-wiki.tag-curator**: Dispatch when one tag surface's axis values need consolidating into a canon (kind=normalize-tags) — a wiki scope or the generated domain-doc tree — from the `tag-tick` schedule or `/lazy-wiki.relink` mid-run. Classifying and See-also linking a single node is the sibling `lazy-wiki.curator`'s job. ([mebius-san/lazy-cortex](https://github.com/mebius-san/lazy-cortex/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/lazycortex-wiki/agents/lazy-wiki.tag-curator.md) · ★1)

- **lb-desktop.agent**: Desktop/Electron platform specialist covering the main process, preload scripts, IPC communication, BrowserWindow management, native menus, and file system access. Use for Electron-specific features and desktop app behavior. ([lichtblick-suite/lichtblick](https://github.com/lichtblick-suite/lichtblick/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/lb-desktop.agent.md) · ★1.2k · 3 copies)
- **lb-docs**: Longbridge Developers 文档编辑。负责 docs/ 下 Markdown 文档的内容撰写、结构组织和多语言同步。当任务涉及 API 文档、SDK 说明、教程或任何 .md 文件的内容编写/修改时激活。 ([longbridge/developers](https://github.com/longbridge/developers/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lb-docs.md) · ★62)
- **lb-engineer**: Use this agent when configuring or tuning load balancers and reverse proxies: NGINX and HAProxy upstreams and balancing algorithms, rate limiting, SSL/TLS termination, keep-alive tuning, AWS ALB/NLB, or Kubernetes ingress controllers (nginx-ingress, Traefik). It sizes the configuration for real production traffic. ([Ormus-Solutions/LibreDevOps-Claude-Code](https://github.com/Ormus-Solutions/LibreDevOps-Claude-Code/blob/HEAD/plugins/load-balancing/agents/lb-engineer.md))
- **lb-extensions.agent**: Extension system specialist covering extension loading, registration, the extension API, contribution points, org vs local namespaces, and the .foxe file format. Use for extension development and extension infrastructure. ([lichtblick-suite/lichtblick](https://github.com/lichtblick-suite/lichtblick/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/lb-extensions.agent.md) · ★1.2k · 3 copies)
- **lb-frontend**: Longbridge Developers 前端工程师。负责 docs/.vitepress/theme/ 下的 Vue 组件和页面开发，遵守 Longbridge 品牌规范与 Shadcn 设计语言的融合风格。当任务涉及新建/修改 Vue 组件、自定义页面、样式调整时激活。 ([longbridge/developers](https://github.com/longbridge/developers/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lb-frontend.md) · ★62)
- **lb-frontend-dev.agent**: General React and TypeScript development specialist for the Lichtblick monorepo. Use for component creation, hooks, state management, styling with tss-react/MUI, and code patterns that don't belong to a specific domain. ([lichtblick-suite/lichtblick](https://github.com/lichtblick-suite/lichtblick/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/lb-frontend-dev.agent.md) · ★1.2k · 3 copies)
